| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Sei dunque pronto a ripudiare la tua famiglia,<br><br><br>a buttar via l’intera tua eredità? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Qual è la mia eredità? Quanto al tuo titolo,<br><br><br>so perché lo prendesti. E la mamma lo sapeva.<br>Primo, perché ti diede l’occasione<br>di ritirarti dalla politica, non senza dignità,<br>non essendo più richiesto. E desideravi essere [[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]]<br>anche per tenere testa alla famiglia della mamma —<br>per spadroneggiare su di loro, in realtà. Oh, non ho [[concepts/doubt\|dubbi]]<br>che il pensiero di trasmettere il tuo nome e il tuo titolo<br>a un figlio ti gratificasse. Ma non era per amor mio!<br><br><br>Ero soltanto tuo figlio — vale a dire,<br>una specie di prolungamento della tua esistenza,<br>un rappresentante che conduce gli affari in tua assenza.<br>Perché dovrei ringraziarti di avermi imposto questo?<br>E che soddisfazione, mi [[concepts/wonder\|chiedo]], te ne verrà<br>nella [[motifs/grave\|tomba]]? Se sarai ancora cosciente dopo la [[motifs/death\|morte]],<br>scommetto che sarà uno stato di coscienza sorpreso.<br>Povero [[archetypes/ghost\|fantasma]]! a fare i conti del suo attivo e passivo<br>e a chiedersi perché si desse pena di simili inezie. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Vuoi dunque che ti aiuti a fuggire da tuo padre! |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | E ad aiutare mio padre a liberarsi di me.<br>Semplicemente non sai quanto più piacevole<br>troverai la vita, una volta che io sia fuori dal paese.<br>Quel che vorrei è la possibilità di andare all’estero<br>come socio in qualche affare interessante.<br>Ma potrebbero chiedermi di mettere del capitale. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Che genere di affare hai in mente? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Oh, non lo so. Importazione ed esportazione,<br><br><br>con la possibilità di guadagnare da entrambe le parti. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Ecco quel che farò per te, [[characters/michael\|Michael]].<br><br><br>Ti aiuterò ad avviarti in qualunque affare<br>tu possa trovare da solo — se, a un esame,<br>sarò soddisfatto della [[concepts/nature\|natura]] dell’affare. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | In ogni caso, sono deciso ad andarmene dall’Inghilterra. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | [[characters/michael\|Michael]]! Ci sono ragioni per cui vuoi andartene<br><br><br>oltre quelle che mi hai detto? Non si tratta… di omicidio colposo? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Omicidio colposo? Perché omicidio colposo? Oh, intendi sulla [[motifs/road\|strada]].<br><br><br>Certamente no. Sono un guidatore fin troppo bravo. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Che cosa allora? Quella giovane donna? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Non sono così [[archetypes/fool\|sciocco]]<br><br><br>da farmi coinvolgere in una causa per rottura di promessa<br>o nel divorzio di qualcuno. No, non devi preoccuparti<br>di quella ragazza — né di nessun’altra.<br>Ma voglio andarmene. Sono stufo dell’Inghilterra. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Sono sicuro che non intendi questo. Ma è abbastanza naturale<br><br><br>voler passare qualche anno all’estero. Potrebbe farti molto bene<br>trovare le tue gambe. Ma non vorrei pensare<br>che a ispirarti non sia stata una [[concepts/ambition\|ambizione]] positiva<br>ma soltanto il [[motifs/desire\|desiderio]] di fuggire. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Non sono un fuggiasco. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | No, non un fuggiasco dalla giustizia —<br><br><br>soltanto un fuggiasco dalla realtà.<br>Oh [[characters/michael\|Michael]]! Se tu avessi qualche meta alta da raggiungere,<br>qualche sogno di eccellenza, con che gioia ti aiuterei!<br>Anche se ti portasse via da me per sempre,<br>a patire il sole monotono dei tropici<br>o a rabbrividire nella [[motifs/night\|notte]] del nord. Credimi, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]:<br>chi fugge dal proprio passato perderà sempre la corsa.<br>Lo so per esperienza. Quando raggiungerai la tua meta,<br>il tuo immaginato paradiso di successo e di grandezza,<br>vi troverai i tuoi fallimenti passati ad accoglierti.<br>Tu sei tutto ciò per cui devo vivere, [[characters/michael\|Michael]] —<br>tu e Monica. Se vivessi vent’anni<br>sapendo che mio figlio ha fatto il vigliacco,<br>starei semplicemente a morire per altri vent’anni. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Molto [[motifs/well\|bene]]: se ti piace, chiamami vigliacco.<br><br><br>Mi [[concepts/wonder\|chiedo]] se tu faresti l’[[archetypes/hero\|eroe]]<br>al posto mio. Non credo che lo faresti.<br>Tu non hai sofferto lo svantaggio che ho avuto io.<br><br><br>Tuo padre era ricco, ma non era nessuno in particolare,<br>e così non avevi nulla all’altezza di cui stare. Quei criteri di condotta<br>di cui hai sempre fatto tanto sfoggio, a mio beneficio:<br>mi [[concepts/wonder\|chiedo]] se tu ne sia sempre stato all’altezza. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [MONICA è entrata inosservata] |  
| MONICA | [[characters/michael\|Michael]]! Come puoi parlare così a papà?<br><br><br>Papà! Che cos’è successo? Perché hai quell’aria adirata?<br>So che [[characters/michael\|Michael]] dev’essere in grossi guai,<br>e allora non puoi aiutarlo? |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Sto cercando di aiutarlo<br><br><br>e di venirgli incontro a metà strada. Gli ho fatto un’offerta<br>che deve meditare. Ma se va all’estero<br>voglio che ci vada in uno spirito molto diverso<br>da quello che ha appena mostrato. |  
| MONICA | [[characters/michael\|Michael]]! Di’ qualcosa. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Che c’è da dire?<br><br><br>Voglio lasciare l’Inghilterra e farmi una carriera mia:<br>e papà si limita a chiamarmi vigliacco. |  
| MONICA | Papà! Tu sai che darei la vita per te.<br><br><br>Oh, come suona sciocca questa frase! Ma non c’è vocabolario<br>per l’[[motifs/love\|amore]] dentro una famiglia, l’[[motifs/love\|amore]] che si abita<br>ma non si guarda, l’[[motifs/love\|amore]] alla cui luce<br>si vede ogni altra cosa, l’[[motifs/love\|amore]] dentro il quale<br>ogni altro [[motifs/love\|amore]] trova parola.<br>Questo [[motifs/love\|amore]] è silenzioso.<br>                                Che cosa posso dirvi?<br>Comunque [[characters/michael\|Michael]] si sia comportato, papà,<br>qualunque cosa papà abbia detto, [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br>dovete perdonarvi l’un l’altro, dovete [[motifs/love\|amarvi]] l’un l’altro. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Avrei potuto amare papà, se lui avesse voluto [[motifs/love\|amore]],<br><br><br>ma non l’ha mai voluto, Monica, non da me.<br>Sai che ti ho sempre voluto molto bene —<br>ho una [[concepts/nature\|natura]] molto affettuosa, davvero,<br>ma… |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra MRS. CARGHILL con una borsa da documenti] |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. [[characters/richard\|Richard]]! Non pensavo che fosse ancora qui.<br><br><br>Ero tornata per leggermi in pace le sue lettere;<br>ma che bello trovare una piccola riunione di famiglia!<br>So chi siete! Lei è Monica, naturalmente:<br>e questo dev’essere suo fratello, [[characters/michael\|Michael]].<br>Ho indovinato, vero? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Sì, ha indovinato.<br><br><br>Ma… |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Come l’ho capito? Perché lei somiglia tanto a suo padre<br><br><br>quando aveva la sua età. È il suo ritratto, [[characters/richard\|Richard]],<br>come lei era una volta. Non deve presentarci,<br>mi presenterò da sola. Io sono Maisie Montjoy!<br>Questo non vi dice nulla, cari miei.<br>È passato tanto [[motifs/time\|tempo]] da quando il nome di Maisie Montjoy<br>era in cima al cartellone della rivista. Ora sono Mrs. John Carghill.<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]]! È stupefacente, questa storia dei suoi figli:<br>Monica non le somiglia quasi per nulla,<br>ma [[characters/michael\|Michael]] — suo padre è cambiato parecchio<br>da quando lo conobbi tanti e tanti anni fa,<br>eppure lei è l’immagine di quel che era allora.<br>Suo padre fu un caro amico mio, una volta. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Somigliava davvero a me? |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Ha la sua voce! e il suo modo di muoversi! È meraviglioso.<br><br><br>E il fascino! Ha ereditato tutto il suo fascino, [[characters/richard\|Richard]].<br>Non si può negarlo. Ma chi è che arriva?<br>È un altro nuovo ospite. Ci sta salutando con la mano.<br>Lo conosce, [[characters/richard\|Richard]]? |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | È un uomo che conoscevo. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Interessante! Ha una gran bella figura<br><br><br>ed è piuttosto esotico d’aspetto. È straniero? |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Viene da qualche parte dell’[[settings/america\|America]] centrale. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Che romantico! Mi piacerebbe [[motifs/love\|tanto]] conoscerlo.<br><br><br>Sta venendo a parlarci. Deve presentarmelo. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Entra GOMEZ] |  
| GOMEZ | Buongiorno, Dick. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Buongiorno, [[characters/fred\|Fred]]. |  
| GOMEZ | Non ti aspettavi che venissi a raggiungerti qui, vero?<br><br><br>Tu sei qui per una cura di riposo. Ho convinto il mio medico<br>che anch’io avevo bisogno di una cura di riposo.<br>E quando ho sentito che avevi scelto di venire a Badgley [[settings/court\|Court]]<br>ho detto al mio medico: «[[motifs/well\|Beh]], che ne dice?<br>Che raccomandazione migliore potrei avere?»<br>E così mi ha mandato qui. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL.          Oh, vi siete visti di recente?<br><br><br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]], credo che potrebbe presentarci. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Oh. Questo è… |  
| GOMEZ | Il suo vecchio amico [[characters/federico-gomez\|Federico Gomez]],<br><br><br>l’eminente cittadino di San Marco.<br>Questo è il mio nome. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Le presento dunque — con quel nome —<br><br><br>la signora… signora… |  
| MRS | CARGHILL.          Mrs. John Carghill. |  
| GOMEZ | Sembriamo un po’ deboli sui cognomi, Dick! |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. [[motifs/well\|Beh]], vede, señor Gomez, quando diventammo amici —<br><br><br>[[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]] e io — mi si conosceva col mio nome d’arte.<br>Ci fu un [[motifs/time\|tempo]], una volta, in cui tutti a [[settings/london\|Londra]]<br>conoscevano il nome di Maisie Montjoy nella rivista. |  
| GOMEZ | Se Maisie Montjoy era bella a vedersi<br><br><br>quanto Mrs. Carghill, capisco [[motifs/well\|bene]]<br>il suo successo sulle scene. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL.                     Oh, non mi ha mai vista?<br><br><br>È un peccato, señor Gomez. |  
| GOMEZ | Avevo perso i contatti con le cose d’Inghilterra.<br><br><br>Se fossi stato a [[settings/london\|Londra]], e nella posizione di Dick,<br>sarei stato il suo più devoto ammiratore. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Non È Troppo Tardi Per [[motifs/love\|Amarmi]]! È la canzone<br><br><br>che fece la mia fama, señor Gomez. |  
| GOMEZ | Non sarà mai troppo tardi. Non sei d’accordo, Dick?<br><br><br>— Questa signorina la prendo per sua figlia?<br>E questo è suo figlio? |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Questo è mio figlio [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br><br><br>e mia figlia Monica. |  
| MONICA | Piacere.<br><br><br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]]! |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Piacere. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Non credo che lei conosca [[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]]<br><br><br>da tanto tempo quanto me, señor Gomez. |  
| GOMEZ | Mia cara signora, lei non è abbastanza vecchia<br><br><br>per aver conosciuto Dick Ferry da tanto tempo quanto me.<br>Eravamo amici a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL.                       Oh, dunque lei è stato a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]!<br><br><br>È così che parla un inglese così perfetto?<br>Naturalmente, ho capito dai suoi tratti che era spagnolo.<br>Gli spagnoli mi piacciono molto. Sono così aristocratici.<br>Ma è molto strano che non ci siamo mai incontrati prima.<br>Lei era amico di [[characters/richard\|Richard]] a [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]<br>e [[characters/richard\|Richard]] e io diventammo grandi amici<br>non molto tempo dopo, vero, [[characters/richard\|Richard]]? |  
| GOMEZ | Immagino che fosse dopo che io avevo lasciato l’Inghilterra. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Certo, questo lo spiega. Dopo [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]<br><br><br>suppongo che lei sia tornato a… dov’è la sua [[motifs/home\|casa]]? |  
| GOMEZ | La repubblica di San Marco. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL.                                   Tornato a San Marco.<br><br><br>Señor Gomez, se è vero che si ferma a Badgley [[settings/court\|Court]],<br>la avverto — le farò un [[motifs/cross\|controinterrogatorio]]<br>e le farò dire tutto di [[characters/richard\|Richard]]<br>ai suoi tempi di [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]. |  
| GOMEZ | A una condizione:<br><br><br>che lei mi dica tutto di Dick quando lo conobbe. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL [dà un colpetto alla borsa]. Segreto per segreto, señor Gomez!<br><br><br>Tocca a lei mettere per primo le carte in tavola! |  
| MONICA | Papà, credo che dovresti riposare adesso.<br><br><br>— Devo spiegare che i medici sono stati molto fermi<br>nel volere che mio padre riposasse in quiete assoluta<br>prima di ogni pasto. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Ma [[characters/michael\|Michael]] e io<br><br><br>dobbiamo continuare la nostra discussione. Questo pomeriggio, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. |  
| MONICA | No, credo che tu abbia parlato abbastanza per oggi.<br><br><br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]], poiché alloggi così vicino,<br>vuoi tornare domattina? Dopo colazione? |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Sì, vieni domattina. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], verrò domattina. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Si ferma qui nei dintorni, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]?<br><br><br>Suo padre è un amico mio così vecchio<br>che mi sembra naturalissimo chiamarla [[characters/michael\|Michael]].<br>Non le dispiace, vero? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | No, non mi dispiace.<br><br><br>Alloggio al George — non è lontano. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Allora mi piacerebbe fare un pezzo di strada con lei. |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Con piacere, certamente. |  
| GOMEZ | In vacanza?<br><br><br>Lei lavora a [[settings/london\|Londra]], vero? |  
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Non in vacanza, no. Ho lavorato a [[settings/london\|Londra]],<br><br><br>ma penso di tagliare i ponti e di andare all’estero. |  
| MRS | CARGHILL. Deve raccontarmi tutto. Forse potrei darle un consiglio.<br><br><br>Ora la lasciamo, [[characters/richard\|Richard]]. Arrivederci, Monica.<br>E señor Gomez, la terrò fede alla sua promessa! |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Escono Mrs. CARGHILL e [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]]] |  
| GOMEZ | [[motifs/well\|Beh]], Dick, dobbiamo obbedire agli ordini dei nostri medici.<br><br><br>Ma finché siamo qui, dobbiamo farci qualche bella chiacchierata<br>sui vecchi tempi. Ciao ciao per adesso. |  
| *(didascalia)* | [Esce] |  
| MONICA | Papà, che gente orribile. Non dobbiamo restare qui.<br><br><br>Voglio che tu fugga da loro. |  
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Quel da cui voglio fuggire<br><br><br>è me stesso, è il passato. Ma che vigliacco sono<br>a parlare di fuggire! E che ipocrita!<br>Pochi minuti fa supplicavo [[characters/michael\|Michael]]<br>di non cercare di fuggire dai propri fallimenti passati:<br>dicevo di saperlo per esperienza. Capisco io il senso<br>della lezione che vorrei insegnare? Su, ricomincio a imparare.<br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]] e io andremo a scuola insieme.<br>Ci siederemo l’uno accanto all’altro, a due banchetti,<br>e patiremo le stesse umiliazioni<br>per mano dello stesso maestro. Ma ho ancora [[motifs/time\|tempo]]?<br>C’è [[motifs/time\|tempo]] per [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. È troppo tardi per me, Monica?<br>SIPARIO |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_3--scene_1" data-title="The Elder Statesman — Act III" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/fool,archetype/ghost,archetype/lover,archetype/outsider,motif/autumn,motif/childhood,motif/death,motif/fire,motif/ghosts,motif/home,motif/lamb,motif/love,motif/memory,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/prison,motif/road,motif/secrets,motif/silence,motif/soul,motif/time,motif/well,concept/conscience,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/freedom,concept/friendship,concept/history,concept/hope,concept/nature,concept/revenge,concept/self,concept/suffering,concept/suicide,concept/truth,form/comedy,form/history,setting/court,setting/london,setting/oxford,character/federico_gomez,character/fred,character/lord_claverton,character/michael,character/monica_claverton_ferry,character/richard"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Same as Act Two. Late afternoon of the following day.MONICA seated alone. Enter CHARLES. |
| CHARLES | [[motifs/well\|Well]], Monica, here I am. I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] you got my message. |
| MONICA | Oh Charles, Charles, Charles, I’m so glad you’ve come!<br><br><br>I’ve been so worried, and rather frightened.<br>It was exasperating that they couldn’t find me<br>When you telephoned this morning. That Mrs. Piggott<br>Should have heard my beloved’s voice<br>And I couldn’t, just when I had been yearning<br>For the sound of it, for the caress that is in it!<br>Oh Charles, how I’ve wanted you! And now I need you. |
| CHARLES | My darling, what I want is to know that you need me.<br><br><br>On that last day in [[settings/london\|London]], you admitted that you loved me,<br>But I wondered … I’m sorry, I couldn’t help wondering<br>How much your words meant. You didn’t seem to need me then.<br>And you said we weren’t engaged yet … |
| MONICA | We’re engaged now.<br><br><br>At least I’m engaged. I’m engaged to you for ever. |
| CHARLES | There’s another shopping expedition we must make!<br><br><br>But my darling, since I got your letter this morning<br>About your father and [[characters/michael\|Michael]], and those people from his past,<br>I’ve been trying to think what I could do to help him.<br>If it’s blackmail, and that’s very much what it looks like,<br>Do you think I could persuade him to confide in me? |
| MONICA | Oh Charles! How could anyone blackmail Father?<br><br><br>Father, of all people the most scrupulous,<br>The most austere. It’s quite impossible.<br>Father with a guilty secret in his past!<br>I just can’t imagine it. |
| *(didascalia)* | [CLAVERTON has entered unobserved] |
| MONICA | I never expected you from that direction, Father!<br><br><br>I thought you were indoors. Where have you been? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Not far away. Standing under the great beech tree. |
| MONICA | Why under the beech tree? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I feel drawn to that spot.<br><br><br>No matter. I heard what you said about guilty [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]].<br>There are many things not crimes, Monica,<br>Beyond anything of which the law takes cognisance:<br>Temporary failures, irreflective aberrations,<br>Reckless surrenders, unexplainable impulses,<br>Moments we regret in the very next moment,<br>Episodes we try to conceal from the world.<br>Has there been nothing in your life, Charles Hemington,<br>Which you wish to forget? Which you wish to keep unknown? |
| CHARLES | There are certainly things I would gladly forget, Sir,<br><br><br>Or rather, which I wish had never happened.<br>I can think of things you don’t yet know about me, Monica,<br>But there’s nothing I would ever wish to conceal from you. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | If there’s nothing, truly nothing, that you couldn’t tell Monica<br><br><br>Then all is [[motifs/well\|well]] with you. You’re in [[motifs/love\|love]] with each other —<br>I don’t need to be told what I’ve seen for myself!<br>And if there is nothing that you conceal from her<br>However important you may consider it<br>To conceal from the rest of the world — your [[motifs/soul\|soul]] is safe.<br>If a man has one person, just one in his life,<br>To whom he is willing to confess everything —<br>And that includes, mind you, not only things criminal,<br>Not only turpitude, meanness and cowardice,<br>But also situations which are simply ridiculous,<br>When he has played the [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] (as who has not?) —<br>Then he loves that person, and his [[motifs/love\|love]] will save him.<br>I’m afraid that I’ve never loved anyone, really.<br>No, I do [[motifs/love\|love]] my Monica — but there’s the impediment:<br>It’s impossible to be quite honest with your [[archetypes/child\|child]]<br>If you’ve never been honest with anyone older,<br>On terms of equality. To one’s [[archetypes/child\|child]] one can’t reveal oneself<br>While she is a [[archetypes/child\|child]]. And by the [[motifs/time\|time]] she’s grown<br>You’ve woven such a web of fiction about you!<br>I’ve spent my life in trying to forget myself,<br>In trying to identify myself with the part<br>I had chosen to play. And the longer we pretend<br>The harder it becomes to drop the pretence,<br>Walk off the stage, change into our own clothes<br>And speak as ourselves. So I’d become an idol<br>To Monica. She worshipped the part I played:<br>How could I be sure that she would [[motifs/love\|love]] the actor<br>If she saw him, off the stage, without his costume and makeup<br>And without his stage words. Monica!<br>I’ve had your [[motifs/love\|love]] under false pretences.<br>Now, I’m tired of keeping up those pretences,<br>But I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] that you’ll find a little [[motifs/love\|love]] in your heart<br>Still, for your father, when you know him<br>For what he is, the broken-down actor. |
| MONICA | I think I should only [[motifs/love\|love]] you the better, Father,<br><br><br>The more I knew about you. I should understand you better.<br>There’s nothing I’m afraid of learning about Charles,<br>There’s nothing I’m afraid of learning about you. |
| CHARLES | I was thinking, Sir — forgive the suspicion —<br><br><br>From what Monica has told me about your fellow guests,<br>Two persons who, she says, claim a very long acquaintance —<br>I was thinking that if there’s any question of blackmail,<br>I’ve seen something of it in my practice at the bar.<br>I’m sure I could help. |
| MONICA | Oh Father, do let him. |
| CHARLES | At least, I think I know the best man to advise you. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Blackmail? Yes, I’ve heard that word before,<br><br><br>Not so very long ago. When I asked him what he wanted.<br>Oh no, he said, I want nothing from you<br>Except your [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] and your company.<br>He’s a very rich man. And she’s a rich woman.<br>If people merely blackmail you to get your company<br>I’m afraid the law can’t touch them. |
| CHARLES | Then why should you submit?<br><br><br>Why not leave Badgley and escape from them? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Because they are not real, Charles. They are merely [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]]:<br><br><br>Spectres from my past. They’ve always been with me<br>Though it was not till lately that I found the living persons<br>Whose [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]] tormented me, to be only human beings,<br>Malicious, petty, and I see myself emerging<br>From my spectral existence into something like reality. |
| MONICA | But what did the [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]] mean? All these years<br><br><br>You’ve kept them to yourself. Did Mother know of them? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Your mother knew nothing about them. And I know<br><br><br>That I never knew your mother, as she never knew me.<br>I thought that she would never understand<br>Or that she would be jealous of the [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]] who haunted me.<br>And I’m still of that opinion. How open one’s heart<br>When one is sure of the wrong response?<br>How make a confession with no [[concepts/hope\|hope]] of absolution?<br>It was not her fault. We never understood each other.<br>And so we lived, with a deep [[motifs/silence\|silence]] between us,<br>And she died silently. She had nothing to say to me.<br>I think of your mother, when she lay dying:<br>Completely without interest in the life that lay behind her<br>And completely indifferent to whatever lay ahead of her. |
| MONICA | It is [[motifs/time\|time]] to break the [[motifs/silence\|silence]]! Let us share your [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]]! |
| CHARLES | But these are only human beings, who can be dealt with. |
| MONICA | Or only [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]], who can be exorcised!<br><br><br>Who are they, and what do they stand for in your life? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | … And yet they’ve both done better for themselves<br><br><br>In consequence of it all. He admitted as much,<br>[[characters/fred\|Fred]] Culverwell … |
| MONICA | [[characters/fred\|Fred]] Culverwell?<br><br><br>Who is [[characters/fred\|Fred]] Culverwell? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | He no longer exists.<br><br><br>He’s [[characters/federico-gomez\|Federico Gomez]], the Central American,<br>A man who’s made a fortune by his own peculiar methods,<br>A man of great importance and the highest standing<br>In his adopted country. He even has sons<br>Following in their father’s footsteps<br>Who are also successful. What would he have been<br><br><br>If he hadn’t known me? Only a schoolmaster<br>In an obscure grammar school somewhere in the Midlands.<br>As for Maisie Batterson … |
| MONICA | Maisie Batterson?<br><br><br>Who is Maisie Batterson? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | She no longer exists.<br><br><br>Nor the musical [[forms/comedy\|comedy]] star, Maisie Montjoy.<br>There is Mrs. John Carghill, the wealthy widow.<br>But Freddy Culverwell and Maisie Batterson,<br>And Dick Ferry too, and [[characters/richard\|Richard]] Ferry —<br>These are my [[motifs/ghosts\|ghosts]]. They were people with good in them,<br>People who might all have been very different<br>From Gomez, Mrs. Carghill and [[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]].<br>Freddy admired me, when we were at [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]];<br>What did I make of his admiration?<br>I led him to acquire tastes beyond his means:<br>So he became a forger. And so he served his term.<br>Was I responsible for that weakness in him?<br>Yes, I was.<br>How easily we ignore the fact that those who admire us<br>Will imitate our vices as [[motifs/well\|well]] as our virtues —<br>Or whatever the qualities for which they did admire us!<br>And that again may nourish the faults that they were born with.<br>And Maisie loved me, with whatever capacity<br>For loving she had — [[concepts/self\|self]]-centred and foolish —<br>But we should respect [[motifs/love\|love]] always when we meet it;<br>Even when it’s vain and selfish, we must not abuse it.<br>That is where I failed. And the [[motifs/memory\|memory]] frets me. |
| CHARLES | But all the same, these two people mustn’t persecute you.<br><br><br>We can’t allow that. What hold have they upon you? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Only the hold of those who know<br><br><br>Something discreditable, dishonourable … |
| MONICA | Then, Father, you should tell us what they already know.<br><br><br>Why should you wish to conceal from those who [[motifs/love\|love]] you<br>What is known so [[motifs/well\|well]] to those who hate you? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I will tell you very briefly<br><br><br>And simply. As for Frederick Culverwell,<br>He re-enters my life to make himself a reminder<br>Of one occasion the [[motifs/memory\|memory]] of which<br>He knows very [[motifs/well\|well]], has always haunted me.<br>I was driving back to [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]. We had two girls with us.<br>It was late at [[motifs/night\|night]]. A secondary [[motifs/road\|road]].<br>I ran over an old man lying in the [[motifs/road\|road]]<br>And I did not stop. Then another man ran over him.<br>A lorry driver. He stopped and was arrested,<br>But was later discharged. It was definitely shown<br>That the old man had died a natural [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>And had been run over after he was dead.<br>It was only a corpse that we had run over<br>So neither of us killed him. But I didn’t stop.<br><br><br>And all my life I have heard, from [[motifs/time\|time]] to [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>When I least expected, between waking and sleeping,<br>A voice that whispered, ‘you didn’t stop!’<br>I knew the voice: it was [[characters/fred\|Fred]] Culverwell’s. |
| MONICA | Poor Father! All your life! And no one to share it with;<br><br><br>I never knew how lonely you were<br>Or why you were lonely. |
| CHARLES | And Mrs. Carghill:<br><br><br>What has she against you? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I was her first [[archetypes/lover\|lover]].<br><br><br>I would have married her — but my father prevented that:<br>Made it worth while for her not to marry me —<br>That was his way of putting it — and of course<br>Made it worth while for me not to marry her.<br>In fact, we were wholly unsuited to each other.<br>Yet she had a peculiar physical attraction<br>Which no other woman has had. And she knows it.<br>And she knows that the [[archetypes/ghost\|ghost]] of the man I was<br>Still clings to the [[archetypes/ghost\|ghost]] of the woman who was Maisie.<br>We should have been poor, we should certainly have quarrelled,<br>We should have been unhappy, might have come to divorce;<br>But she hasn’t forgotten or forgiven me. |
| CHARLES | This man, and this woman, who are so vindictive:<br><br><br>Don’t you see that they were as much at fault as you<br>And that they know it? That’s why they are inspired<br>With [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] — it’s their means of [[concepts/self\|self]]-justification.<br>Let them tell their versions of their miserable stories.<br>Confide them in whispers. They cannot harm you. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Your reasoning’s sound enough. But it’s irrelevant.<br><br><br>Each of them remembers an occasion<br>On which I ran away. Very [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>I shan’t run away now — run away from them.<br>It is through this meeting that I shall at last escape them.<br>— I’ve made my confession to you, Monica:<br>That is the first step taken towards my [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]],<br>And perhaps the most important. I know what you think.<br>You think that I suffer from a morbid [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>From brooding over faults I might [[motifs/well\|well]] have forgotten.<br>You think that I’m sickening, when I’m just recovering!<br>It’s hard to make other people realise<br>The magnitude of things that appear to them petty;<br>It’s harder to confess the sin that no one believes in<br>Than the crime that everyone can appreciate.<br>For the crime is in relation to the law<br>And the sin is in relation to the sinner.<br>What has made the difference in the last five minutes<br>Is not the heinousness of my misdeeds<br>But the fact of my confession. And to you, Monica,<br>To you, of all people. |
| CHARLES | I grant you all that.<br><br><br>But what do you propose? How long. [[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]],<br>Will you stay here and endure this persecution? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | To the end. The place and [[motifs/time\|time]] of liberation<br><br><br>Are, I think, determined. Let us say no more about it.<br>Meanwhile, I feel sure they are conspiring against me.<br>I see Mrs. Carghill coming. |
| MONICA | Let us go. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | We will stay here. Let her join us. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter MRS. CARGHILL] |
| MRS | CARGHILL. I’ve been hunting high and low for you, [[characters/richard\|Richard]]!<br><br><br>I’ve some very exciting news for you!<br>But I suspect … Dare I? Yes, I’m sure of it, Monica!<br>I can tell by the change in your expression to-day;<br>This must be your fiancé. Do introduce him. |
| MONICA | Mr. Charles Hemington. Mrs. Carghill. |
| CHARLES | How do you do. |
| MRS | CARGHILL.                  What a charming name! |
| CHARLES | I’m glad my name meets with your approval, Mrs. Carghill. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. And let me congratulate you‚ Mr. Hemington.<br><br><br>You’re a very lucky man, to get a girl like Monica.<br>I take a great interest in her future.<br>Fancy! I’ve only known her two days!<br>But I feel like a mother to her already.<br>You may say that I just missed being her mother!<br>I’ve known her father for a very long [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>And there was a moment when I almost married him,<br>Oh so long ago. So you see, Mr. Hemington,<br>I’ve come to regard her as my adopted daughter.<br>So much so, that it seems odd to call you Mr. Hemington:<br>I’m going to call you Charles! |
| CHARLES | As you please, Mrs. Carghill. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | You said you had some exciting news for us.<br><br><br>Would you care to impart it? |
| MRS | CARGHILL.                         It’s about dear [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Oh? What about [[characters/michael\|Michael]]? |
| MRS | CARGHILL.                                                 He’s told me all his story.<br><br><br>You’ve cruelly misunderstood him, [[characters/richard\|Richard]].<br>How he must have suffered! So I put on my thinking cap.<br>I know you’ve always thought me utterly brainless,<br>But I have an idea or two, now and then.<br>And in the end I discovered what [[characters/michael\|Michael]] really wanted<br>For making a new start. He wants to go abroad!<br>And find his own way in the world. That’s very natural.<br>So I thought, why not appeal to Señor Gomez?<br>He’s a wealthy man, and very important<br>In his own country. And a friend of [[characters/michael\|Michael]]’s father!<br>And I found him only too ready to help. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | And what was Señor Gomez able to suggest? |
| MRS | CARGHILL. Ah! That’s the surprise for which I’ve come to prepare you.<br><br><br>Dear [[characters/michael\|Michael]] is so happy — all his problems are solved;<br>And he was so perplexed, poor [[motifs/lamb\|lamb]]. Let’s all rejoice together. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Enter GOMEZ and [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]]] |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], [[characters/michael\|Michael]], you know I expected you this morning,<br><br><br>But you never came. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | No, Father. I’ll explain why. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | And I learn that you have discussed your problems<br><br><br>With Mrs. Carghill and then with Señor Gomez. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | When I spoke, Father, of my wish to get abroad.<br><br><br>You couldn’t see my point of view. What’s the use of chasing<br>Half round the world, for the same sort of job<br>You got me here in [[settings/london\|London]]? With another Sir Alfred<br>Who’d constitute himself custodian of my morals<br>And send you back reports. Some sort of place<br>Where everyone would sneer at the fellow from [[settings/london\|London]],<br>The limey remittance man for whom a job was made.<br>No! I want to go where I can make my own way,<br>Not merely be your son. That’s what Señor Gomez sees.<br>He understands my point of view, if you don’t.<br><br><br>And he’s offered me a job which is just what I wanted. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Yes, I see the advantage of a job created for you<br><br><br>By Señor Gomez … |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | It’s not created for me.<br><br><br>Señor Gomez came to [[settings/london\|London]] to find a man to fill it,<br>And he thinks I’m just the man. |
| GOMEZ | Yes, wasn’t it extraordinary. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Of course you’re just the man that Señor Gomez wants,<br><br><br>But in a different sense, and for different reasons<br>From what you think. Let me tell you about Gomez.<br>He’s unlikely to try to be custodian of your morals;<br>His real name is Culverwell … |
| GOMEZ | My dear Dick,<br><br><br>You’re wasting your [[motifs/time\|time]], rehearsing ancient [[concepts/history\|history]].<br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]] knows it already. I’ve told him myself.<br>I thought he’d better learn the facts from me<br>Before he heard your distorted version.<br>But, Dick, I was nettled by that insinuation<br>About my not being custodian of [[characters/michael\|Michael]]’s morals.<br>That is just what I should be! And most appropriate,<br>Isn’t it, Dick, when we recall<br>That you were once custodian of my morals:<br><br><br>Though of course you went a little faster than I did. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | On that point, [[characters/fred\|Fred]], you’re wasting your [[motifs/time\|time]]:<br><br><br>My daughter and my future son-in-law<br>Understand that allusion. I have told them the story<br>In explanation of our … intimacy<br>Which they found puzzling. |
| MRS | CARGHILL.                       Oh,[[characters/richard\|Richard]]!<br><br><br>Have you explained to them our intimacy too? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I have indeed. |
| MRS | CARGHILL.                           The romance of my life.<br><br><br>Your father was simply irresistible<br>In those days. I melted the first [[motifs/time\|time]] he looked at me!<br>Some day, Monica, I’ll tell you all about it. |
| MONICA | I am satisfied with what I know already, Mrs. Carghill,<br><br><br>About you. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. But I was very lovely then. |
| GOMEZ | We are sure of that! You’re so lovely now<br><br><br>That we can [[motifs/well\|well]] imagine you at … what age were you? |
| MRS | CARGHILL. Just eighteen. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Now, [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br><br><br>Señior Gomez says he has told you his story.<br>Did he include the fact that he served a term in [[motifs/prison\|prison]]? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | He told me everything. It was his experience<br><br><br>With you, that made him so understanding<br>Of my predicament. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | And made him invent<br><br><br>The position which he’d come to find the man for. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | I don’t care about that. He’s offered me the job<br><br><br>With a jolly good screw, and some pickings in commissions.<br>He’s made a fortune there. San Marco for me! |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | And what are your duties to be? Do you know? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | We didn’t go into details. There’s [[motifs/time\|time]] for that later. |
| GOMEZ | Much better to wait until we get there.<br><br><br>The [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of business in San Marco<br>Is easier explained in San Marco than in England. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Perhaps you intend to change your name to Gomez? |
| GOMEZ | Oh no, Dick, there are plenty of other good names. |
| MONICA | [[characters/michael\|Michael]], [[characters/michael\|Michael]], you can’t abandon your family<br><br><br>And your very [[concepts/self\|self]] — it’s a kind of [[concepts/suicide\|suicide]]. |
| CHARLES | [[characters/michael\|Michael]], you think Señor Gomez is inspired by benevolence — |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | I told you he’d come to [[settings/london\|London]] looking for a man<br><br><br>For an important post on his staff — |
| CHARLES | A post the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of which is left very vague |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | It’s confidential, I tell you. |
| CHARLES | So I can imagine:<br><br><br>Highly confidential … |
| GOMEZ | Be careful, Mr. Barrister.<br><br><br>You ought to know something about the law of slander.<br>Here’s Mrs. Carghill, a reliable witness. |
| CHARLES | I know enough about the law of libel and slander<br><br><br>To know that you are hardly likely to invoke it.<br>And, [[characters/michael\|Michael]], here’s another point to think of:<br>Señor Gomez has offered you a post in San Marco,<br>Señor Gomez pays your passage … |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | And an advance of salary. |
| CHARLES | Señor Gomez pays your passage … |
| GOMEZ | Just as many years ago<br><br><br>His father paid mine. |
| CHARLES | This return of past kindness<br><br><br>No [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] gives you pleasure? |
| GOMEZ | Yes, it’s always pleasant<br><br><br>To repay an old debt. And better late than never. |
| CHARLES | I see your point of view. Can you really feel confidence,<br><br><br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]], in a man who aims to gratify, through you,<br>His lifelong grievance against your father?<br>Remember, you put yourself completely in the power<br>Of a man you don’t know, of the [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of whose business<br>You know nothing. All you can be sure of<br>Is that he served a [[motifs/prison\|prison]] sentence for forgery. |
| GOMEZ | [[motifs/well\|Well]], [[characters/michael\|Michael]], what do you say to all this? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | I’ll say that Hemington has plenty of cheek.<br><br><br>Señor Gomez and I have talked things over, Hemington … |
| GOMEZ | As two men of the world, we discussed things very frankly;<br><br><br>And I can tell you, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]’s head is [[motifs/well\|well]] screwed on.<br>He’s got brains, he’s got flair. When he does come back<br>He’ll be able to buy you out many times over. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. [[characters/richard\|Richard]], I think it’s [[motifs/time\|time]] I joined the conversation.<br><br><br>My late husband, Mr. Carghill, was a business man —<br>I wish you could have known him, Señor Gomez!<br>You’re very much alike in some ways —<br>So I understand business. Mr. Carghill told me so.<br>Now, [[characters/michael\|Michael]] has great abilities for business.<br>I saw that, and so does Señor Gomez.<br>He’s simply been [[concepts/suffering\|suffering]], poor boy, from frustration.<br>He’s been waiting all this [[motifs/time\|time]] for opportunity<br>To make use of his gifts; and now, opportunity —<br>Opportunity has come knocking at the door.<br>[[characters/richard\|Richard]], you must not bar his way. That would be shameful. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I cannot bar his way, as you know very [[motifs/well\|well]].<br><br><br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]]’s a free agent. So if he chooses<br>To place himself in your power, [[characters/fred\|Fred]] Culverwell,<br>Of his own volition to contract his enslavement,<br>I cannot prevent him. I have something to say to you,<br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]], before you go. I shall never repudiate you<br>Though you repudiate me. I see now clearly<br>The many many mistakes I have made<br>My whole life through, mistake upon mistake,<br>The mistaken attempts to correct mistakes<br>By methods which proved to be equally mistaken.<br>I see that your mother and I, in our failure<br>To understand each other, both misunderstood you<br>In our divergent ways. When I think of your [[motifs/childhood\|childhood]],<br>When I think of the happy little boy who was [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br>When I think of your boyhood and adolescence,<br>And see how all the efforts aimed at your good<br>Only succeeded in defeating each other,<br>How can I feel anything but sorrow and compunction? |
| MONICA | Oh [[characters/michael\|Michael]], remember, you’re my only brother<br><br><br>And I’m your only sister. You never took much notice of me.<br>When we were growing up we seldom had the same friends.<br>I took all that for granted. So I didn’t know till now<br>How much it means to me to have a brother. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Why of course, Monica. You know I’m very fond of you<br><br><br>Though we never really seemed to have much in common.<br>I remember, when I came [[motifs/home\|home]] for the holidays<br>How it used to get on my nerves, when I saw you<br>Always sitting there with your nose in a book.<br>And once, Mother snatched a book away from you<br>And tossed it into the [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. How I laughed!<br>You never seemed even to want a flirtation,<br>And my friends used to chaff me about my highbrow sister.<br>But all the same, I was fond of you, and always shall be.<br>We don’t meet often, but if we’re fond of each other,<br>That needn’t interfere with your life or mine. |
| MONICA | Oh [[characters/michael\|Michael]], you haven’t understood a single word<br><br><br>Of what I said. You must make your own life<br>Of course, just as I must make mine.<br>It’s not a question of your going abroad<br>But a question of the spirit which inspired your decision:<br>If you wish to renounce your father and your family<br>What is left between you and me? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | That makes no difference.<br><br><br>You’ll be seeing me again. |
| MONICA | But who will you be<br><br><br>When I see you again? Whoever you are then<br>I shall always pretend that it is the same [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. |
| CHARLES | And when do you leave England? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | When we can get a passage.<br><br><br>And I must buy my kit. We’re just going up to [[settings/london\|London]].<br>Señor Gomez will attend to my needs for that climate.<br>And you see, he has friends in the shipping line<br>Who he thinks can be helpful in getting reservations. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. It’s wonderful, Señor Gomez, how you manage everything!<br><br><br>— No sooner had I put my proposal before him<br>Than he had it all planned out! It really was an inspiration —<br>On my part, I mean. Are you listening to me, [[characters/richard\|Richard]]?<br>You look very distrait. You ought to be excited! |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Is this good-bye then, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], that just depends.<br><br><br>I could look in again. If there’s any point in it.<br>Personally, I think that when one’s come to a decision,<br>It’s as [[motifs/well\|well]] to say good-bye at once and be done with it. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Yes, if you’re going, and I see no way to stop you,<br><br><br>Then I agree with you, the sooner the better.<br>We may never meet again, [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | I don’t see why not. |
| GOMEZ | At the end of five years he will get his first leave. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]]… there’s nothing more to say, is there? |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Nothing at all. |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Then we might as [[motifs/well\|well]] be going. |
| GOMEZ | Yes, we might as [[motifs/well\|well]] be going.<br><br><br>You’ll be grateful to me in the end, Dick. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. A parent isn’t always the right person, [[characters/richard\|Richard]],<br><br><br>To solve a son’s problems. Sometimes an [[archetypes/outsider\|outsider]],<br>A friend of the family, can see more clearly. |
| GOMEZ | Not that I deserve any credit for it.<br><br><br>We can only regard it as a stroke of good fortune<br>That I came to England at the very moment<br>When I could be helpful. |
| MRS | CARGHILL. It’s truly providential! |
| MONICA | Good-bye [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. Will you let me write to you? |
| GOMEZ | Oh, I’m glad you reminded me. Here’s my business card<br><br><br>With the full address. You can always reach him there.<br>But it takes some days, you know, even by air mail. |
| MONICA | Take the card, Charles. If I write to you, [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br><br><br>Will you ever answer? |
| [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] | Oh of course, Monica.<br><br><br>You know I’m not much of a correspondent;<br>But I’ll send you a card, now and again,<br>Just to let you know I’m flourishing. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Yes, write to Monica. |
| GOMEZ | [[motifs/well\|Well]], good-bye Dick. And good-bye Monica.<br><br><br>Good-bye, Mr…. Hemington. |
| MONICA | Good-bye [[characters/michael\|Michael]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exeunt [[characters/michael\|MICHAEL]] and GOMEZ] |
| MRS | CARGHILL. I’m afraid this seems awfully sudden to you, [[characters/richard\|Richard]];<br><br><br>It isn’t so sudden. We talked it all over.<br>But I’ve got a little piece of news of my own:<br>Next [[motifs/autumn\|autumn]], I’m going out to Australia,<br>On my doctor’s advice. And on my way back<br>Señor Gomez has invited me to visit San Marco.<br>I’m so excited! But what pleases me most<br>Is that I shall be able to bring you news of [[characters/michael\|Michael]].<br>And now that we’ve found each other again,<br>We must always keep in touch. But you’d better rest now.<br>You’re looking rather tired. I’ll run and see them off. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit MRS. CARGHILL] |
| MONICA | Oh Father, Father, I’m so sorry!<br><br><br>But perhaps, perhaps, [[characters/michael\|Michael]] may learn his lesson.<br>I believe he’ll come back. If it’s all a failure<br>Homesickness, I’m sure, will bring him back to us;<br>If he prospers, that will give him confidence —<br>It’s only [[concepts/self\|self]]-confidence that [[characters/michael\|Michael]] is lacking.<br>Oh Father, it’s not you and me he rejects,<br>But himself, the unhappy [[concepts/self\|self]] that he’s ashamed of.<br>I’m sure he loves us. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Monica my dear,<br><br><br>What you say comes [[motifs/home\|home]] to me. I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] for [[characters/michael\|Michael]];<br>Nevertheless, you are right to [[concepts/hope\|hope]] for something better.<br>And when he comes back, if he does come back,<br>I know that you and Charles will do what you can<br>To make him feel that he is not estranged from you. |
| CHARLES | We will indeed. We shall be ready to welcome him<br><br><br>And give all the aid we can. But it’s both of you together<br>Make the force to attract him: you and Monica combined. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | I shall not be here. You heard me say to him<br><br><br>That this might be a final good-bye.<br>I am sure of it now. Perhaps it is as [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| MONICA | What do you mean, Father? You’ll be here to greet him.<br><br><br>But one thing I’m convinced of: you must leave Badgley [[settings/court\|Court]]. |
| CHARLES | Monica is right. You should leave. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | This may surprise you: I feel at peace now.<br><br><br>It is the peace that ensues upon contrition<br>When contrition ensues upon knowledge of the [[concepts/truth\|truth]].<br>Why did I always want to dominate my children?<br>Why did I mark out a narrow path for [[characters/michael\|Michael]]?<br>Because I wanted to perpetuate myself in him.<br>Why did I want to keep you to myself, Monica?<br>Because I wanted you to give your life to adoring<br>The man that I pretended to myself that I was,<br>So that I could believe in my own pretences.<br>I’ve only just now had the illumination<br>Of knowing what [[motifs/love\|love]] is. We all think we know,<br>But how few of us do! And now I feel happy —<br>In spite of everything, in defiance of reason,<br>I have been brushed by the wing of happiness.<br>And I am happy, Monica, that you have found a man<br>Whom you can [[motifs/love\|love]] for the man he really is. |
| MONICA | Oh Father, I’ve always loved you,<br><br><br>But I [[motifs/love\|love]] you more since I have come to know you<br>Here, at Badgley [[settings/court\|Court]]. And I [[motifs/love\|love]] you the more<br>Because I [[motifs/love\|love]] Charles. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Yes, my dear.<br><br><br>Your [[motifs/love\|love]] is for the real Charles, not a make-believe,<br>As was your [[motifs/love\|love]] for me. |
| MONICA | But not now, Father!<br><br><br>It’s the real you I [[motifs/love\|love]] — the man you are,<br>Not the man I thought you were. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | And [[characters/michael\|Michael]] —<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] him, even for rejecting me,<br>For the me he rejected, I reject also.<br><br><br>I’ve been freed from the [[concepts/self\|self]] that pretends to be someone;<br>And in becoming no one, I begin to live.<br>It is worth while dying, to find out what life is.<br>And I [[motifs/love\|love]] you, my daughter, the more truly for knowing<br>That there is someone you [[motifs/love\|love]] more than your father —<br>That you [[motifs/love\|love]] and are loved. And now that I [[motifs/love\|love]] [[characters/michael\|Michael]],<br>I think, for the first [[motifs/time\|time]] — remember, my dear,<br>I am only a beginner in the practice of loving —<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], that is something.<br>                                           I shall leave you for a while.<br>This is your first visit to us at Badgley [[settings/court\|Court]],<br>Charles, and not at all what you were expecting.<br>I am sorry you have had to see so much of persons<br>And situations not very agreeable.<br>You two ought to have a little [[motifs/time\|time]] together.<br>I leave Monica to you. Look after her, Charles,<br>Now and always. I shall take a stroll. |
| MONICA | At this [[motifs/time\|time]] of day? You’ll not go far, will you?<br><br><br>You know you’re not allowed to stop out late<br>At this season. It’s chilly at dusk. |
| [[characters/lord-claverton\|LORD CLAVERTON]] | Yes, it’s chilly at dusk. But I’ll be warm enough.<br><br><br>I shall not go far. |
| *(didascalia)* | [Exit CLAVERTON] |
| CHARLES | He’s a very different man from the man he used to be.<br><br><br>It’s as if he had passed through some door unseen by us<br>And had turned and was looking back at us<br>With a glance of farewell. |
| MONICA | I can’t understand his going for a walk. |
| CHARLES | He wanted to leave us alone together! |
| MONICA | Yes, he wanted to leave us alone together.<br><br><br>And yet, Charles, though we’ve been alone to-day<br>Only a few minutes, I’ve felt all the [[motifs/time\|time]] … |
| CHARLES | I know what you’re going to say!<br><br><br>We were alone together, in some mysterious fashion,<br><br><br>Even with [[characters/michael\|Michael]], and despite those people,<br>Because somehow we’d begun to belong together,<br>And that awareness … |
| MONICA | Was a shield protecting both of us … |
| CHARLES | So that now we are conscious of a new person<br><br><br>Who is you and me together.<br>                                                    Oh my dear,<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] you to the limits of speech, and beyond.<br>It’s strange that words are so inadequate.<br>Yet, like the asthmatic struggling for breath,<br>So the [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] must struggle for words. |
| MONICA | I’ve loved you from the beginning of the world.<br><br><br>Before you and I were born, the [[motifs/love\|love]] was always there<br>That brought us together.<br>                                             Oh Father, Father!<br>I could speak to you now. |
| CHARLES | Let me go and find him. |
| MONICA | We will go to him together. He is close at hand.<br><br><br>Though he has gone too far to return to us.<br>He is under the beech tree. It is quiet and cold there.<br>In becoming no one, he has become himself.<br>He is only my father now, and [[characters/michael\|Michael]]’s.<br>And I am happy. Isn’t it strange, Charles,<br>To be happy at this moment? |
| CHARLES | It is not at all strange.<br><br><br>The dead has poured out a blessing on the living. |
| MONICA | Age and decrepitude can have no terrors for me,<br><br><br>Loss and vicissitude cannot appal me,<br>Not even [[motifs/death\|death]] can dismay or amaze me<br>Fixed in the certainty of [[motifs/love\|love]] unchanging.<br>                                                                         I feel utterly secure<br>In you; I am a part of you. Now take me to my father.<br>CURTAIN<br>The Cast of the First Production<br>at the<br>Edinburgh Festival<br>August 25–August 30 1958<br><br><br>[[characters/monica-claverton-ferry\|Monica Claverton-Ferry]]<br>ANNA MASSEY<br>Charles Hemington<br>[[characters/richard\|RICHARD]] GALE<br>Lambert<br>GEOFFREY KERR<br>[[characters/lord-claverton\|Lord Claverton]]<br>PAUL ROGERS<br>[[characters/federico-gomez\|Federico Gomez]]<br>WILLIAM SQUIRE<br>Mrs. Piggott<br>DOROTHEA PHILLIPS<br>Mrs. Carghill<br>EILEEN PEEL<br>[[characters/michael\|Michael]] Claverton-Ferry<br>ALEC MCCOWEN<br>Presented by HENRY SHEREK<br>Directed by E. MARTIN BROWNE<br>Settings designed by HUTCHINSON SCOTT<br><br>APPENDIX POEMS WRITTEN IN EARLY YOUTH |
